ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u17	There you go. I would like to inform you that in the discussion on this item, we will hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Rafał Weber, Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u18	Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! By representing the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I will present our position towards the government's draft law amending the Act on the Military Property Agency, print No 841, and the report of the National Defence Committee, which is the result of the meeting of the Commission on 22 September this year, print No 866. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports this government submission, recognising that the proposed changes will contribute to the improvement of the functioning of the Military Property Agency and to the wider exploitation of the Agency's potential by increasing the capacity to provide services to or supervised by the national defence minister's organisational units. Here it should be mentioned that now on the basis of the article. 53 of the Military Property Agency Act, the Agency may provide services only and exclusively to the Minister of National Defence, i.e. this provision excludes the provision of such services to military units. The amendments to the draft law aim to ensure that the Agency and its subsidiaries can provide specialised services related, for example, to critical infrastructure. Such a solution ensures the safety of the provision of sensitive services, e.g. construction of ammunition warehouses or other elements of military infrastructure. We therefore understand the government's intention to carry out such important tasks as are carried out by an entity under the full control of the Minister of National Defence. Another important element of the amendment to this Act is the proposal for solutions that will improve the functioning of the Agency: the election of the president and deputy president of the Military Property Agency will take place on the same basis as in other executive agencies, e.g. Agricultural Real Estate Agency or Agricultural Market Agency. The proposed provisions provide that the President of the Military Property Agency will appoint and dismiss the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister for National Defence. It is worth mentioning that the amendment of the contested law gives the opportunity, on the basis of a decision of the Minister of National Defence or the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, to transfer or dispose of military equipment or dual-use items and arms, as well as to dispose of military equipment or dual-use items and of strategic importance for the maintenance of international peace and security, which are in the hands of organisational units subordinate to the Minister of National Defence or the Minister competent for Home Affairs. The proposed amendments are designed to reduce the time involved in deciding whether to transfer or dispose of military equipment or dual-use items free of charge. Previous practice in this area indicates that negotiation processes related to the conclusion of international agreements are too time-consuming and that the situation sometimes requires immediate action. The maintenance of decision-making procedures and processes may lead to the loss of the ability of foreign entities to dispose of military equipment or dual-use items and sometimes to take over a given contract or market. In conclusion, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, recognising the proposed changes as justified, supports the government's submission together with legislative amendments which were voted on at the committee meeting. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Barbara Dolniak Sejm]]

In the explanatory memorandum of the bill, we can read that the analysis of the functioning of the Military Property Agency has shown that there are many problems that need to be solved by statutory changes. It is very interesting to recognise that the current problem is how to appoint the President of the AMW and its deputies. The Government intends to enable the President of the Council of Ministers, at the request of the Minister for National Defence, to appoint and dismiss the President and thus to resign from recruitment in an open and competitive competition. In addition, in accordance with the wording of the draft Act, the Vice-President of the Agency will be appointed and cancelled by the Minister of Defence. As if this were not enough, the amendment of the current law provides for the Minister of Defence to be given the national competence to determine the remuneration not only to the President of the Agency and its alternates, but also to the President and to the other members of the Supervisory Board. If the chimney bill goes down, the forks will be gone, fixing the salary. Consequently, these salaries can be determined in any way possible and will be in accordance with the Act. Therefore, the entire authority over the Military Property Agency will rest in the hands of the Minister of Defence. As we go on, we read that the proposed lifting of the mouth. Article 2 28 aims at establishing a single annual financial plan within the Agency, thereby eliminating the financial autonomy of the directors of the regional branches of the agencies. This record clearly indicates a change in direction towards centralisation - instead of the current decentralisation - the functioning and level of decision-making of the various regional departments of the AMW. In conclusion, the most important changes that the government intends to make in relation to the current Military Property Agency Act, it should be clearly pointed out that the change in the current democratic principles of the entity's operation in favour of the authoritarian decisions of the Minister of Defence cannot and probably will not produce positive results. For future agency and supervisory staff, this will be a clear signal that competence, knowledge and skills are not as important as party membership. The Commission shall, in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall, at the request of the applicant, submit the following request from a minority: In Article 7, point 3 is replaced by the following: Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 is replaced by the following: "1. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for the purpose of considering a draft law amending the Law on the departments of government administration and some other laws - as I said earlier - held two meetings. A number of amendments were adopted in the course of the work, with the vast majority being clear and legislative. They most often referred to the technical means of making changes, as well as the location of the amended rules. In several cases, the amendments were also intended to supplement the provisions authorising guidance on the content of the implementing act. An amendment was adopted to change the participation of the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport in the adoption of the Regulation pursuant to Article. 49 Dangerous Goods Transport Act. As a result of the amendments adopted, amendments to the Law on the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the Law on the Principles for the Financing of Science were waived. Also as a result of the amendment adopted, the article was supplemented. 19 Act on ports and marinas for a provision according to which the representatives of the municipalities in the supervisory boards of companies: the Management Board of the Maritime Port of Gdansk SA, the Management Board of the Maritime Port of Gdynia SA and the Management Board of Maritime Ports of Szczecin and Świnoujście SA shall not constitute more than 1/5 of the composition of the board if the municipalities have not taken up shares representing at least 10% of the shares the total share capital of the company concerned, each municipality should have at least one representative on the supervisory board of the relevant company regardless of the size of the shares. On the other hand, no amendment was adopted to limit the changes to the article. 19 Law on ports and marinas only to point 1, i.e. the amendment of minorities, which I presented earlier. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u56	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the draft law amending the Law on the departments of government administration and certain other laws, print No 861. The Parliamentary draft law amending the Law on the departments of government administration and certain other laws concerns the creation of ministers competent for maritime, fisheries and inland navigation legal instruments necessary for the proper performance of the tasks entrusted to them. The state policy on these three divisions requires a deep reconstruction, and on many issues of reconstruction - reconstruction of the Polish shipbuilding industry, reconstruction of the Polish shipping market and the Polish inland fleet, as well as the development of a new fisheries development strategy. A number of amendments were adopted during the drafting of the Act in the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs. The vast majority of them were purely legislative, it was simply a different way of making changes, but also a substantive one, among others. The aim was to supplement the guidelines in accordance with constitutional requirements. In the course of the work in committee at first reading, the article was deleted. 20 and Article 20 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 37, on the delegation of part of the competence to oversee a certain section of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. The applicants, in consultation with the Ministry of Agriculture and the Ministry of Maritime Affairs, agreed to simply abandon this transfer and to leave the ARiMR unchanged. Article 1 is also deleted. 21 amending the Law on the Principles for the Financing of Science. Here, in turn, the issue concerned the exclusion of part of the competence from the area of the Minister for Science in the field of research. As previously, the consultation of the applicants with the ministries led to conclusions, however, in order to leave these regulations unchanged, hence the deletion of Article. 21. This law on amending the Law on Government Administrations is another step towards strengthening the state's policy to promote the development of maritime economy, the development of shipyards, ports, fishing and inland waterway transport, which can become a flywheel of the Polish economy. High Izbo! The Ministry of Maritime Affairs, created in the times of Law and Justice in 2005-2007, was unfortunately abolished by the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party, and unfortunately its achievements were largely wasted. Maritime affairs, inland waterway transport have been dispersed between the ministries of infrastructure, treasure, economy, environmental protection and many others, which have not been treated as it should have been, have not been treated with the utmost care. Today we see that the rebuilt Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping has proved successful, we see that it is successful. However, additional tools must be provided to ensure that these important issues are developed as soon as possible for our economy. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice requests that the Act amending the Government Departments Act and certain other laws from print No 861 be adopted. Thank you.

It is of course the right of every government to shape the system according to the needs of government plans. In fact, if it weren't for some of the proposed regulations, it would be natural for this project to be supported by our club. Unfortunately, like other such submissions addressed by the High House in recent months, it has significant shortcomings preventing, at least at this moment, voting in favour of its adoption. And so the first question concerns the authorship of this project. It is a parliamentary project, not a government project, but in fact it should be the result of government practice and in our view it should be presented to the High House by the government. Why is this so important? Even during the project process, it turned out that the proposed rules were not in line with the government's view on the issue of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. The project originally provided that, for this agency, the minister responsible for fisheries would have almost equal rights as the minister responsible for rural development. I feel like it was part of some fractal struggle inside the ruling party. Similarly, although, of course, on a smaller scale, the proposed amendment to the Law on the Financing of Science - the Minister for Maritime Affairs - was to be some sort of consultant in terms of the amount of resources allocated to research in the field of maritime, inland waterway and fisheries, which in my view could have resulted in permanent blockage of decisions. Another example of the failure of the Parliamentary project was the constitutional question expressed by the State Treasury's Public Prosecutor's Office regarding the regulation of the issuing of implementing acts in the field of transport law, namely their impreciseness due to generality. Of course, this was improved during the committee's work. However, the examples I have cited lead to the conclusion that it is indeed up to the government to submit a draft amendment to the Law on administrative departments, as this is a kind of range of activities of individual ministers and it is difficult for the High House to share competences among individual ministers. I regret to say that the position we share with the President of the Polish Academy of Sciences, which draws attention to the changes in the Act on the National Centre for Research and Development, which are causing a disruption of representativeness between scientific, economic and government administration, is still valid. Despite wide support for the request to maintain the appropriate proportion, the government and the Members of the applicant failed to take into account the conclusions of the position of the Polish State. Therefore, the government administration will be privileged in the Council and we believe that this could undermine the quality of the Council's findings and its politicalisation. There are still serious doubts about the changes that the project introduced in the Education Act. They entrust the Minister with power over the establishment and running of public maritime, inland waterway and fisheries schools, thereby limiting the competence of the district government in the field of vocational education. Meanwhile, the opinion of the Polish County Association is absolute in this matter. These schools already existed, and they were inefficient, that's the wrong way. Although, of course, I understand and even welcome the proposal that competition curricula should be evaluated by ministries, although generally based on guidance from industry entrepreneurs. I appreciate that, because in this field of vocational education, our Ministry of Education... I'll leave three dots. Finally, I would like to express my critical opinion on the article. 6 and Article 6 40. Art. 6 is a typical stool jump in companies of the State Treasury, whereas art. 40 is a typical staff article. Unfortunately, the Bear action. en did not teach the government side or members of the parliamentary majority anything. I have no illusions that you still want to fire competent people and hire your officers. It's ruining our country. High Izbo! In view of my position, I would like to inform you that the Modern Posel Club cannot support the submission as presented by the committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

It is about including its role in laws that concern these companies, e.g. companies related to ports and marinas. The third group deals with changes in transport security laws, exceptional conditions, increased security and clarification of rules. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u66	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members' Circle of Free and Solidarni, I present my position on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the law on the departments of government administration and certain other laws after work in committee. The aim of the proposed regulation was to strengthen the Polish maritime economy. The measures that would lead to the implementation of the proposed concepts are, among others. transfer from other ministries to the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport for the proper fulfilment of the tasks assigned to him. However, as a result of the consultation between the resorts, the original scope of the powers which the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport would have obtained as a result of the adoption of that law was reduced. The functioning of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture has been left in its current form and part of the competence of the Minister responsible for Science has been excluded from the field of research. As these changes are the result of discussions between the ministries concerned, we have no objection to them. In addition to this, a number of amendments have been introduced in the committee's work by the applicants, which should not raise any doubts. These are among others. changes in the area of examinations confirming professional qualifications governing the form of approval of the composition of the examination boards and the catalogue of questions, the identification of implementing acts which would be no longer valid in connection with the amendments introduced by the law, and other amendments of an editorial and clarification nature. In our view, there should also be no change in the personal compositions of the supervisory boards of public limited liability companies referred to in the article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 3 laws on ports and marinas. According to this regulation, representatives of municipalities may not constitute more than 1/5 of the composition of that board if the municipalities do not have at least 10% shares in the total share capital of the company concerned. At the same time, it is noted that at least one representative of the municipality must be in the Council. Such a solution will avoid a situation where the fate of the company is decided by the Supervisory Board, where the majority are representatives of a municipality with a negligible amount of shares in its total share capital. This regulation will also contribute to the greater contribution of the municipality to the development of the company, not only the decision-making but also the capital contribution. In view of the fact that, despite many changes, this draft law after work in committee still meets the original assumptions of strengthening and restoring the maritime economy that we have supported from the outset, the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the contested law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[We invite you to read the bill.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u82	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the joint committees: the Committee on Public Finance, the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies on the Government's draft law amending certain laws defining the conditions for conducting innovative activities. This draft was examined by the Sejm in its first reading at its meeting on 13 September and addressed to these joint committees. On 14 September, the committees set up a special subcommittee to work on a project I had the honour of chairing, and on 22 September we presented a report to the joint committees on the work of the subcommittee. The committees have adopted the report I am presenting today. Two words of general nature about the project. Ladies and gentlemen, this project, although it has not so far aroused a greater interest in the media, is a project, I would say a key, fundamental one, because it responds to this fundamental problem that we have and which we will have in a few years' time, especially in the case of the Polish economy, the exhaustion of a certain functional model, or perhaps rather dysfunctional economic development. What is his character? First of all, it is characterised by the fact that it is based on cheap labour, on the exploitation of natural resources, moreover, on the exploitation of robbery, carried out under such conditions that this resource, the Poles, did not renew itself. We all know the demographic collapse we're currently in. Young Poles forced to work more than their own at low rates, often under bad conditions, not on the basis of a contract of employment, elected emigration or, while staying in the country, did not feel confident enough to start a family, buy an apartment, ensure their family's existence, so that also those families would expand. So that so far our economic model - I will repeat this because this is a very important issue - was based on the robbing operation of an unrenewed natural resource. We have to change that. We need to move on to the economy in which Poles earn well, under stable conditions, which ties them to Poland and makes them want to bind their future to it, to start families and live here, and in order to achieve this, we need, among others. move towards a knowledge-based economy, innovation, high-quality services and products. How does it look today? Not so good. Poland committed to increase R &amp; D expenditure to 1,7% GDP in 2020 However, in 2014 it was only 0.94% , of which almost half are in private companies. In the EU's innovation ranking, we have a long 24th place, and in the global innovation competitiveness ranking we are at 72nd place. We can therefore say that, so far, the existing innovation support mechanisms are insufficient, and particularly insufficient incentives are available to encourage action in the area of: research and development in the private sector. That is why it is necessary - and that is the aim of this project - to construct a series of tools, a series of instruments that will reward such innovative activities. It naturally involves certain risks, of course, with certain costs, so entrepreneurs may often not be willing to bear these risks. That is why we need to build a tax system and a whole legal environment that will support and promote these activities. The subcommittee and committees adopted a total of three substantive amendments and a number of drafting amendments for the project at their meetings. I will now present these substantive amendments, briefly presenting the principles of the law. In the course of this, I will refer to the amendments adopted. What are the main assumptions here? Firstly, it is a change in the income tax laws which will exclude from the catalogue the taxable value revenue corresponding to the import to companies in the form of intellectual capital, i.e. from now on companies that will be created to commercialise to implement innovative products, ideas, patents or such cases... If a person joins such a company by bringing in his intellectual value, then his income will not be taxed. We also extend the catalogue of eligible costs to R &amp; D. They can be deducted from the tax base. We increase the amount of these deductions and, above all, simplify them for small and medium-sized enterprises. This will avoid costly accounting and cumbersome separation of costs, because so far these deductions have varied for different types of costs, and now for those so-called ones. Teddy bears, i.e. small and medium-sized enterprises, are unified. The first change - I will signal - which we have worked in committee, is to exclude these tax reductions from the scope of R &amp; D from the catalogue of permitted public aid according to EU regulations. Without this, they would involve a number of additional bureaucratic and reporting harassments. The comparative analysis of solutions in other European countries, as well as the decisions of the European institutions in this respect, shows that we can exclude them from this and not treat them as permitted state aid. We extend from 3 to 6 years the settlement period of these expenditures. Another very important issue. In the case of start-ups, i.e. companies that are just starting their business and may still not earn enough revenue from their activities, i.e. in order to be able to benefit fully from this increased R &amp; D allowance, a mechanism for reimbursement of eligible cash costs has been introduced. We are also deregulating low value public property management for the benefit of research, science and universities, so that they can obtain additional revenue from their activities more quickly and more easily. We are introducing the possibility of funding funds for environmental protection and water management projects related to the environmental technology verification system. Here again within the committee, an amendment has been introduced to allow GreenEvo to be supported, which is the implementation of the NIK audit recommendations. Another important change is the exclusion from the governance regime of the Treasury's assets requiring the approval of the Minister of the Treasury of cases where universities carry out commercialisation of the subject matter of their research, e.g. through technology transfer centers. The so-called procedure is simplified and verified. the patronage of scientists, i.e. the acquisition by scientific staff of rights to the results of their research and development. We extend the periods in which researchers have the right to participate in the benefits of commercialisation of their work, make the ownership of inventions created by the financing of research by the NCBR more flexible, and finally introduce a minimum level of expenditure for the commercialisation of research results. Here, too, I signal a substantive change made at committee meetings. Well, it was originally this. 0,5% , we increase it to 2% appropriations to finance the maintenance of research capacity. We want to encourage and encourage, and even, I would say, a little bit of pressure on scientific institutes to translate the results of their research into real economic benefits, that is, to say the least, simply commercialised. In conclusion, I would like to add that the bill was very efficient, with the support of all political forces in committee and subcommittees, above divisions. The amendments were usually submitted unanimously, without any comments. The final content of the report was also recommended unanimously by the committees. Finally, I would like to inform you that the joint committees recommend that the High House adopt the draft as set out in the report. Thank you very much.

There it was pointed out that we adopted about such an investor model, that investors usually withdraw from companies between 3 and 5 years of investment, because then they begin to have the greatest impact. So we decided to give an additional year for a certain guarantee, knowing that in mature innovative systems this ageing process is faster. However, in our country, this ecosystem is only being built and this period may be a little longer. Mr Piotr Misilo asked about the risks. Thank you very much for this question, because this is actually a key element of the whole investment process concerning innovative solutions. We have mentioned this many times and we encourage... In general, this is a question that concerns a very important topic, namely the laws can create a good law, a friendly climate, but they do not always create a good practice. What will now be the concern of ladies and gentlemen of Members, is that we should be able to use these records - in this sense, I return to the topic of risk - also to encourage officials - but not only officials, also university administration, the administration of research institutes - to be willing to take the risks associated with the use of the instruments presented in this law. The government's position on this issue is clear - we should introduce tolerance for failure in our dictionary. Innovative projects in most cases end in failure, but in those cases where they do not end in failure, they guarantee success, which compensates for failure. The logic by which we say that each spent gold must turn is appropriate, but only for the whole process, not for individual projects. So we have to sum it up at the end, see what the results are, not account for every single project. With regard to questions that concern financial issues, since a certain innovation is applied in the case of this law, as you know, the position of the government is presented by two ministries, I will allow myself not to answer these questions and leave them to the person with the right characteristics. Once again, thank you very much for your cooperation with this law and for the unanimous adoption of the report of the Joint Committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Strong leadership must yield to the ability to manage a team of people, where there is room for very diverse types of personality and ways of receiving the outside world. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u111	Thank you very much, Member. On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, Mrs Bożena Henczyca. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u112	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I would like to present our position on amending the Social Security Act, print No.803. According to Art. Article 84 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 13 October 1998 on the social security system, the person who has received an undue social security benefit shall be obliged to pay it back. According to the abovementioned above. the law may not be required to reimburse the amounts of social security benefits unduly collected for more than the last 12 months if the person receiving the benefit has notified the paying authority of the circumstances leading to the cessation of the right to benefits or the suspension of their payment, and yet the benefits have still been paid and in other cases for more than 3 years. In the current legal state, the provisions provide only for a period for which the pension authority may require the refund of the benefits unduly collected from the person who received the benefit, i.e. 3 years back from the last payment of the benefit. On the other hand, it does not provide for a period after which the Social Insurance Institution is unable to claim the refund of an unduly collected benefit. It follows from the Act that the claims for the wrongly received benefits are valid after 10 years, but the above period starts only from the date on which the decision fixing those claims is valid. Consequently, the pension authority shall not be limited by any time limit for a decision requiring the reimbursement of the benefits unduly collected. The provisions providing for the reimbursement of unduly collected benefits are as reasonable as possible, but there is a reasonable need to indicate the period during which it would be possible to make a decision allowing the recovery of the unduly collected benefit. In view of the above, it is proposed to amend the statute governing the institution of limitation as regards the reimbursement of unduly collected benefits in such a way that a decision requiring the reimbursement of the amount of unduly collected social security benefits may not be issued later than 5 years after the last day of the period for which undue benefits have been claimed. The expected effect of the entry into force of the regulation will be to protect the certainty and stability of the legal order and to exclude claims from the pension body, which would be separated from the actual situation of the insured person in view of the significant expiry of the period from the date on which they were created at the time of their completion. The Civic Platform Club is in favour of the amendment to the Social Security Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u113	Thank you very much. Please take the floor of Mr Jarosław Porwich from the Kukiz Club15. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u114	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, I have the honour to present my position on the government draft law amending the Social Security Act, print No.803. The proposed amendment concerns the Social Insurance Act, specifically the introduction in the provisions relating to the issue of the unduly collected provision of a limitation of the pension body, in so far as it is possible to make a decision requiring the reimbursement of the benefits unduly collected. The current rules only provide for a period for which the pension authority may require the person who received the benefits unduly to be reimbursed, i.e. three years back from the last payment, i.e. the receipt of the benefit. In addition, Article 2 is replaced by the following: The 84th Act on the social security system shows that claims for unduly collected benefits are valid after 10 years, but the expiry of the above period starts only from the date of the decision establishing those claims. Consequently, the pension authority shall not be limited by any time limit for a decision requiring the reimbursement of benefits unduly collected. It may be issued at any time, even 10 years after the person is unduly entitled to benefits. The amendment to the Act proposes an amendment by specifying that a decision requiring reimbursement of the amount of social security benefits unduly collected may not be issued later than 5 years after the last day of the period for which the benefits were unduly collected. The introduction of such a temporary limitation in the case of the possibility of a decision requiring the reimbursement of an unduly collected social security benefit disciplines the pension authority, affects the strengthening of the legal order and thus constitutes a decisive pro-Citizen element. Therefore, the Kukiz Club15 considers it appropriate to introduce such a solution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u118	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Article 84 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Act of 13 October 1998 on the social security system (i.e.: Dz. U. z 2015 poz. 121, zm.). The reason for the amendment is the judgment of the Constitutional Court, which concluded that the above-mentioned judgment was of the opinion. the article is incompatible with the basic law. The judges indicated in the statement of reasons that the Social Insurance Institution, as a specialised public body, should carry out its control tasks in the field of social security without undue delay, since it has the appropriate tools to achieve this objective. At the same time, it was considered that the Social Insurance Institution was not too eager to enforce claims, although on the other hand it was always very meticulous - the reason: although the main amount was symbolic, interest was no longer. The reason for the problems of insured persons was that the Social Insurance Institution started to count the statute only after its decision was made, and this is because the funds for unduly collected benefits are subject to an statute of 10 years from the date of the decision establishing those entitlements. This also meant that the insurance was practically in an impossible situation. Moreover, the legislator did not specify the time between the payment of the last of these benefits and the date by which the authority may issue the above decision. In practice, the proposed amendment will mean protecting the certainty and stability of the legal order and excluding claims from the pension body which, in view of the significant expiry of the period from the date of their creation at the time of completion, would be separated from the actual situation of the insured person. Therefore, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in favour of this law. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u132	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs a position on the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Print No 857 on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Employment Promotion and Employment Market Institutions and the Act on Pre-retirement Benefits from Print No 804. This short but very important amendment, meeting the expectations of carers of persons with disabilities, gives the possibility to acquire the right to unemployment benefits and the pre-retirement benefit by persons who have received care or special care benefits in connection with the care of a disabled person. The purpose of the proposed measures is due to the fact that we want to treat the period of care for a disabled person in a similar way to the period of employment. The provision of benefits or benefits to the caretaker of a disabled person is a partial compensation for termination of employment, and therefore it is appropriate to treat this form of activity similarly to employment as regards the possibility of receiving unemployment benefits or retirement benefits after the loss of care benefits for the disabled person. The explanatory memorandum to the bill states: Social and demographic considerations are also in favour of the proposed solution. In the absence of a systemic solution to the identified issues of care for disabled persons, the ageing and sick take-over by those close to care and the dismissal of work, which would otherwise be possible for carers, should be appreciated and supported by sufficient State aid. The direction of the changes contained in the bill is positive because it is prosocial, but one can also ask itself why this privilege will be granted only if the loss of the right to this benefit or the benefit is due to the death of the person over whom care was exercised. There are also various other situations leading to the loss of these benefits, not just the death of a disabled person. It must be made clear that the proposed solutions do not constitute sufficient State aid, as stated in the explanatory memorandum. It's just a drop in the sea of needs. We believe that people taking care of people with disabilities should be dealt with in a comprehensive way. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u162	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a report by the Commission for European Union Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft law on ratification of the enhanced partnership and cooperation agreement between the European Union and its Member States, of the one part, and the Republic of Kazakhstan, of the other part, drawn up at Astana on 21 December 2015, print No 817. The bill entered the Sejm on 25 July this year. It concerns the consent of the Polish Sejm to ratify the agreement with Kazakhstan by the President of the Republic of Poland. The ratification of the agreement is intended to reflect efforts to deepen cooperation in three key areas: cooperation in foreign and security policy, economic cooperation, sectoral cooperation, financial and technical cooperation and cooperation in migration, environment, transport, education, IT, agriculture, rural development and others. The enhanced partnership and cooperation agreement between the European Union and its Member States, of the one part, and the Republic of Kazakhstan, of the other part, shall enter into force on the first day of the second month following the date on which the last party informs the depositary of the completion of the legal procedures necessary for this purpose. This is a mixed agreement. Until its entry into force, it must be approved by the European Union only after the ratification of the Agreement by all Member States and the Republic of Kazakhstan. The ratification procedures of the Agreement have already been completed by the Republic of Kazakhstan and by the European Union - Latvia. The entry into force of the agreement vis-à-vis Poland will not result in the need to change Polish law, it will not have social or financial consequences. The agreement should activate bilateral economic contacts and social dialogue and thus increase the economic presence of Polish companies in Kazakhstan. On 22 September this year. first reading was carried out at the joint committee meeting. After the discussion, and the discussion concerned not only the cooperation of the European Union with Kazakhstan, Poland and Kazakhstan, but also the situation of Kazakhstan itself, democracy in Kazakhstan, we also had the opportunity to talk about the recent visit of the President of Kazakhstan, Mr Nazarbayev, in Poland and the resulting agreements concerning our cooperation. Commissions, joint committees... After considering the draft law, the Joint Committees: for European Union and Foreign Affairs unanimously recommend that the draft law be adopted without amendments. The committee's report is contained in print No 869. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u164	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The signing of the agreement with Kazakhstan on 21 December 2015 confirms the vitality and relevance of the eastern dimension of the European Union's foreign policy and, within it, creates a field for strengthening the political dialogue between Poland and Kazakhstan and implementing the priorities of Polish foreign policy. It will complement Poland's active policy towards the European Union's eastern neighbours in Central Asia, notably as part of a wide range of activities related to the Eastern Partnership. The ratification of the agreement will reflect efforts to deepen cooperation in three key areas determining the specific role of Kazakhstan in the European Union's relations with the region of Central Asia. The first point is the issue of political dialogue and cooperation in the field of foreign and security policy. Kazakhstan, aspiring to the role of regional leader, is very active in the international arena, including the political dialogue between Kazakhstan and the European Union. The issue of Russian-Chinese relations in the context of Russia's deteriorating relations with the West will be an important factor affecting the situation of Kazakhstan. The importance and role of China in the Kremlin policy seems to be growing, which could lead Russia to take into account China's strategic interests in Central Asia and may prove beneficial for Kazakhstan. From the perspective of Kazakhstan, China, and not the West, are the only factor capable of mitigating Russia in Central Asia and thus providing Astana with a room for manoeuvre in relations with the Kremlin. The test for Russian-Chinese relations will be the fate and nature of the interaction between the Chinese concept of the transport corridor The new Silk Road and the Russian Eurasian Union, the key element of which is Kazakhstan. The third point is economic cooperation. Kazakhstan is a country with the largest economy in the Central Asia region, characterised since the 2005 economic crisis by a stable, few percent growth in GDP, huge raw material resources and a large potential which seems to be a challenge for our side. I will mention here another aspect of this agreement, which is the issue of sectoral cooperation. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u188	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 7 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the excise duty Act (print No 849). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Wiesław Jasiński to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u189	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present a brief outline of the justification for the amendment to the excise duty Act, print No 849. This amendment affects the amendment of the Article. 31a of a law which provides for excise duty exemption for coal products. This amendment is, in principle, a combination of points 8 and 9 of the article into one point. As a result, it will exempt coal products intended for fuel use by an energy-intensive undertaking using coal products, in which a system has been put in place to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. Two independent exemptions of coal products are currently in force: firstly, intended for heating purposes by an energy-intensive undertaking using coal products, without requiring that plant to implement the principles leading to environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency, and secondly, intended for heating purposes by an economic operator in which a system leading to environmental objectives has been put into effect or to increase energy efficiency. These exemptions were challenged by the European Commission. In the Commission's view, the exemption currently in force has a wider scope than that resulting from the Article. 17 Directives on the restructuring of the Community framework for the taxation of energy products and electricity. According to Union legislation, energy products used by energy-intensive undertakings may be exempt from tax provided that agreements and other systems leading to environmental objectives or energy efficiency gains are in place for those plants, which are broadly equivalent to those achieved if the standard Community minimum rates are respected. At this point, it should be noted that the excise duty law for gas products, which is similar to that proposed in the amendment, is in force. Implementation of the amendments in this respect will prevent the European Commission from initiating formal proceedings against Poland under Article. 258 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and will not expose Poland to financial penalties. In summary, this amendment will have an impact on two groups of actors. The first group are entities which are not energy-intensive but have a system in place to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. This group will not benefit from excise duty exemption after the proposed regulations have been introduced. The second group of operators are those that are energy-intensive but do not have a system in place to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. The new wording will require those operators to introduce a system to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency in order to continue to benefit from excise duty exemption. It was assumed that this amendment could involve annual revenue for the State budget of PLN 25 million. In view of the above, it is justified to introduce the proposed amendment to the excise duty law. Marshal, thank you very much for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u190	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marcin Porzucek, the Law and Justice Club, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u191	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The European Commission has challenged the scope of excise duty exemptions for coal products used by energy-intensive undertakings and by economic operators in which a system has been put in place to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. The provisions in this respect were introduced by the Law of 16 September 2011 on the reduction of certain obligations of citizens and entrepreneurs. On the occasion of this particular project, which concerns excise duty, it is worth leaning on the issue of European energy policy and the directions chosen, because the changes in question relate to energy issues, coal on which our economy is still based and which is one of the guarantors of our energy security. The new regulations will allow energy-intensive undertakings to continue to benefit from exemptions, but after the introduction of measures to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. According to this idea, redundancies operate in other countries of the European Union. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u196	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, a club of the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u197	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I will present my position on the government's proposal to amend the excise duty Act, print No. 849. The project discussed today is a very short change in excise duty exemption for coal products used by energy-intensive companies. This draft is the result of the European Commission questioning the provisions adopted by the High House in which exemption from excise duty for coal products intended for heating purposes is included in two cases. As the Minister stressed, these are two independent redundancies. First, when dealing with an energy-intensive undertaking using coal products, the second concerns an economic operator, not necessarily an energy-intensive entity, in which a system has been put into effect to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. The European Commission has considered these provisions to be too broad and incompatible with Directive 2003/96/EC. The draft amendment concerns Article. 31a of the Excise Tax Act and in principle consists of combining points 8 and 9 into one point. The change will result in a reduction of excise duty exemption - only for energy-intensive entities which at the same time introduce a system for achieving environmental objectives or increasing energy efficiency. The date of entry into force of the Act was set at 1 July 2017 in order to allow time for stakeholders to adapt to the new regulations. By eliminating certain entities from excise duty exemption, the revenue of the state budget will increase by PLN 25 million per year, as the government states in the explanatory memorandum. I would also like to point out that the planned changes will be detrimental to the agri-food sector, especially the meat industry and the dairy sector. In the opinion of the Union Polish Meat planned change will result in about 10% businesses in both sectors will increase their business costs. At the same time, it is calculated that a typical medium-sized company with a monthly production of 1.5 million kg will have to add to each kilogram of coal product 1 gr, resulting in an average cost increase of 12 thousand. PLN per month. In small companies it will be over 4,000. zł. In turn, in the opinion of the Institute of Agricultural Economy, the abolition of the exemption from excise duty will affect thousands of small Polish agricultural producers all over Poland who use coal boilers in agricultural production. As a result, an increase in the cost of agricultural production is to be expected, and these are to be thought to be added to the price of their products, which will result in an increase in retail food prices for consumers. In conclusion, it is precisely for these reasons that we are dissatisfied with the proposed amendments eliminating exemptions, which must be considered necessary because of the compatibility of our law with EU legislation. Therefore, the club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of transferring the project to the committee. Thank you very much.

We proceed with the examination of item 8 of the agenda: First reading of the Government's draft law amending the law on the application for an infringement of the party's right to hear the case in preparatory proceedings conducted or supervised by the prosecutor and judicial proceedings without undue delay and some other laws (print No 851). I ask the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Justice of Minister Marcin Warchoł to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u207	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The project is the implementation of the ruling of the European Court of Human Rights in the Rutkowski case and others against Poland, in which the Court pointed out that the problem of chronicity is a systemic problem. It's so-called. last year's pilot judgment, which concerns only those complainants, but formally, concerns a number of other complaints, nearly 600 complaints which are pending trial at the Strasbourg Court and which have been brought in the last 3 years. The Court stated to the Polish side that this problem, as I said, is a systemic problem, which means that the government needs to deal quickly with all these matters on a national level. There is therefore a problem with the case law of the Polish courts in the context of the case law of the European Court of Human Rights, namely that there is a discrepancy between the national case law and the case law of the Strasbourg Court. First of all, the Polish courts carry out the so-called unlawful actions. fragmentation of the procedure, i.e. analyze the duration only in the context of a specific section, the section of the procedure, without looking at the proceedings in its entirety, is limited to the last stage at which the complaint was brought for the duration. That's one thing. Secondly, they point out that the amount of the money allocated is too low, which is precisely due to the lack of an overall assessment. We have therefore proposed to modify a number of provisions of the Act concerning the complaint for chronicity. First of all, first of all, we change the criteria for assessing the concept of "diagnosing the case within a reasonable period' in such a way that consideration is subject to the whole period, i.e. we end with the so-called. fragmentation, the whole period, from the beginning to the moment of the complaint. Secondly, we exclude the possibility for the State Treasury to file a complaint. Thirdly, we point out that the court competent to hear the complaint is also competent to hear the complaint as regards the length of the proceedings, because these are two different moments: judicial proceedings and preparatory proceedings. Fourthly, we mitigate the formal requirements for this complaint, eliminate the requirement to quote the facts justifying the request - it is estimated that more than 8.5 thousand. complaints shall be rejected only on grounds of non-compliance with the formal requirements. Finally, we guarantee an adequate monetary sum in the context compensation in order to make this real in relation to the damage suffered by the party concerned as a result of that duration, which means that the sum of money will never be less than 2 000. zł, and if the procedure lasts relatively longer, we increase it by another 1 000 in the following years. zł. And these are the main assumptions of this first point of this project. The second point concerns the disciplinary responsibility of the Judges and their declaration of property. The analysis of legislation on this issue revealed the need for a number of legislative actions, including: implementation of the recommendations contained in the report of the Group of States against the Corruption of the Council of Europe - GRECO. It is therefore proposed to introduce a regulation whereby the information contained in the judge's property statement is to be made public, in line with the rules on prosecutors. Secondly, doubts are eliminated as to the nature of the liability for wilful misrepresentation in a property statement made by ordinary courts' judges by introducing a provision providing for criminal liability for misrepresentation or concealing the truth in a property statement. Thirdly, the period of disciplinary proceedings is extended, specifically the limitation period of disciplinary deliberation. Fourthly, a new disciplinary penalty is introduced in the form of a reduction in the basic salary of a judge from 5% to 15% for the period from 6 months to 2 years. At the same time, the judgment of each disciplinary penalty, in addition to the warning, will be accompanied by the deprivation of the possibility of being promoted to a higher judicial position for 5 years, the inability to participate in the college of the court, the judgment of the disciplinary court and the taking up of the office of President, Vice President or Head of the local branch in court. The third group of proposed changes concerns the situation of prosecutors. It is proposed to introduce a regulation whereby the participation of the Attorney General or his deputy is mandatory in meetings of the entire NSA. It is proposed that the National Prosecutor's Office or the Public Prosecutor's Office of another agency delegated to act in the National Public Prosecutor's Office should attend the meeting of seven judges of the NSA. It will also be the case with regard to the meetings of the Supreme Court and the Constitutional Tribunal. Ratio legis. There is no reasonable reason for prosecutors to be posted to the National Public Prosecutor's Office to be excluded from carrying out their tasks. In addition, according to the principle of inferencing in the prosecution, there is no rule that a particular prosecutor is taking action, but it is a representative of the prosecutor's office as a whole. And finally, there are changes in disciplinary proceedings against prosecutors. Here we propose that the files of cases legally completed be made available on the basis set out in the Act on access to public information. So it's about the transparency of disciplinary proceedings, the issue, the idea that NGOs, the Ombudsman, have been calling for for for a long time. Finally, the last point, the fifth point, concerns issues relating to the return of the case to the prosecutor. The return shall be made at the request of the prosecutor and the prosecutor if his application is not taken into account, he must be able to carry out the necessary preparatory proceedings without delay. These activities are largely detective. Any delay could lead to distortion or loss of evidence, not to mention the fact that there is a high risk of action of the nature of litigation by those concerned. It is therefore proposed to waive the possibility to challenge the decision of the court to refer the case to the prosecutor in this situation. Finally, the last group are transitional provisions. They concern limitation periods, statements of property of judges and the last point I have pointed out here, namely the return of the case to the prosecutor in particularly justified situations. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u209	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The proposal submitted by the Government to amend the Act on complaint against a party's right to investigate a case in pre-trial proceedings conducted or supervised by a prosecutor and judicial proceedings without undue delay and certain other laws met with positive acceptance in the environment of Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. The need for legislative work on these provisions is due to the need to implement the ruling of the European Court of Human Rights. The European Court of Human Rights pointed out the appropriateness of the national authorities to take appropriate legislative action to ensure that the case law of national courts which examines long-term complaints complies with established standards resulting from the provisions of the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. Having regard to the above, in the draft Article. 1 paragraph 3 First of all, the Act provides that the Act is to apply in accordance with the standards resulting from the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, drawn up in Rome on 4 November 1950. The purpose of the Act is to emphasise that the Act is to be applied taking into account its requirements. The proposed amendment of the Law on Complaints aims to prevent courts from limiting the recognition of a complaint only to the stage at which the proceedings are to be carried out, which is to be achieved by modifying the criteria for assessing the determination of the case within a reasonable period of time. Furthermore, the amendment takes into account the Court's guidelines to guarantee an adequate amount of money for breaching the party's right to hear the case without undue delay. This shall be achieved through the obligation of the courts to take account of this sum of the entire length of the proceedings, from the time of initiation until the time when the action is dealt with. It is also proposed to introduce a regulation whereby the information contained in the judge's property statement is to be made public, in line with the rules on prosecutors. The introduction of the disclosure of the judges' property statements aims to strengthen confidence in the courts as an institution and to the judges themselves. The proposal also aims to remove inconsistencies in disciplinary proceedings, in particular the possibility of terminating those proceedings without imposing a disciplinary penalty before the expiry of the limitation period. The amendment will constitute an implementation of the recommendations of the Council of Europe Group of States Against Corruption, recommending measures to ensure that disciplinary proceedings relating to the misconduct of judges and prosecutors end before the expiry of the limitation period, such as an appropriate extension of the limitation period or interruption, or suspension of the limitation period in certain circumstances. On the other hand, the proposed amendment to abolish separate limitation periods for disciplinary and disciplinary decisions and the extension of the limitation period is relatively minor and is, in fact, based on the rationalisation of the applicable law. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is 1 The Law on the System of General Courts after point 2 is proposed to add point 2a containing a new disciplinary penalty, as the Minister mentioned, to reduce the basic remuneration of the judge from 5% to 15% for a period from 6 months to 2 years. A wider catalogue of penalties is intended to provide greater flexibility in their application. In addition, the proposal introduces different effects of certain disciplinary penalties than has been the case so far. The decision of any disciplinary penalty, other than an injunction, will result in the deprivation of the possibility of being promoted to higher judicial positions for a period of 5 years, the impossibility of taking part in the college of the court, the judgment in the disciplinary court and the appointment of the President of the court, the Vice-President of the court, the head of the court centre. The extension of the catalogue of penalties will allow greater possibilities for the disciplinary court to react to professional misconduct. It cannot be denied that the proposed changes will contribute both to improving justice work and simply to increasing citizens' confidence in those who hold the weight and the sword of Tamida in their hands. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u211	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law amending the law on the application for an infringement of the right of the party to investigate the case in the preparatory proceedings conducted or supervised by the prosecutor and judicial proceedings without undue delay and certain other laws, print No 851. The bill consists of two completely separate parts. The first part, according to the title, concerns amendments to the complaint law resulting from the need to implement the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights of July 2015 in the Rutkowski case and others against Poland. The European Court of Human Rights pointed out in its ruling that the national authorities should take appropriate legislative action to ensure that the case-law of the national courts determining actions for chronicity is consistent with established standards resulting from the provisions of the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. The Court's judgment was delivered under the so-called procedure. a pilot judgment, which means that it concerns nearly 600 complaints about the same issue brought against Poland in the last 3 years. Taking into account the Court's view, the draft law amends the system of assessing whether the proceedings have taken place in the long term by taking account of the cumulative time taken from the initiation of the proceedings until the time when the action was examined, irrespective of at which stage the action was brought. The amendment also takes into account the Court's guidelines on the adequacy of the amount of the monetary sum for the duration of the proceedings. An overall assessment of the procedure and flat-rate minimum compensation amounts shall be introduced. This part of the bill does not raise much doubt and should be examined as an independent document under consideration in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and in the Committee on European Union Affairs. The inclusion in this draft of amendments to other laws which have absolutely no common characteristics is unacceptable. It also runs counter to the principles of correct legislation. This is an attempt to introduce highly controversial and dangerous regulations to the justice system through the kitchen door. The introduction of the disclosure of judges' property claims undermines the role of the state as an independent justice system. It's a tool to put pressure on the independence of a judge to be protected. If the objective is, as we read in the explanatory memorandum, to fight corruption, then this idea is missed and the goal will not be achieved. In most European countries, judges do not make statements of property at all, and corruption is close to zero. That's the way it is. Finland. Let us take advantage of their experience, and let us not resort to restrictive methods which, in extreme cases, will cause the accused person to judge the property statement of the judge handling the case and to take an interest in its assets. I strongly stress that the current system of statements of property of judges is sufficient to assess their reliability and reliability. In view of the above, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform requests that the bill be rejected at first reading. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u217	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I would like to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the law on the application for an infringement of the party's right to investigate a case in the preparatory proceedings conducted or supervised by the prosecutor and judicial proceedings without undue delay and certain other laws. The proposal provides for the revision of a number of legal acts, but particularly major amendments concern the Law on the System of Universal Courts. First of all, we are talking about the obligation to disclose the statements of property of judges and to publish them in the Public Information Bulletin, second, about changes in disciplinary proceedings against judges and prosecutors regarding the disclosure of statements of property of judges. Incomprehensible to me are the voices of the judge's community saying that this poses a threat to the independence of judges or to their personal safety. However, I do not consider it crucial to argue from the justification of the project that the introduction of the disclosure of the judges' property statements is intended to strengthen confidence in the courts as an institution and to the judges themselves. You can argue with whether this will be the effect of this change. More important, in my opinion, is the argument that persons with the status of a public official should make such statements openly. Only secret, secret service officers may be excluded, e.g. CBA or CBI officers. The judges are acting openly, so that limitation should not apply to them. In addition, a security has been introduced whereby, at the request of a Judge who has made a declaration, an entity entitled to receive a declaration may decide to include the information contained in the security statement provided for classified information: reserved, as laid down in the provisions of the Act of 5 August 2010 on the protection of classified information, if disclosure of that information would endanger the judge or the persons closest to him. The amendment of the Law on the System of General Courts is also intended to eliminate doubts as to the nature of the liability for the deliberate misrepresentation in the property declaration made by the General Court's judges. The rules so far do not specify whether such conduct constitutes a criminal offence or only a disciplinary measure. This results in systemic inconsistencies, taking into account the provision of the Article. 14 the Law of 21 August 1997 on the limitation of the conduct of business by persons performing public functions, as well as Article. Article 35 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 9th Act of 9 May 1996 on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator. Those provisions provide that a statement of property shall be made under criminal liability and in this case standards relating to ordinary court judges should not be lower than those applicable to other persons performing public functions. The extension of the limitation period from 3 to 5 years, as well as from the initiation of proceedings, to 8 years, should be read in the case of disciplinary proceedings for plus. In addition, in the article. 109 § 1 after point 2 of the Act, the applicant proposes to add point 2a containing a new disciplinary penalty, i.e. reduction of the basic salary of a court judge in general from 5% to 15% for a period from 6 months to 2 years. There is also an obligation to impose a combined penalty in the event of simultaneous punishment for several disciplinary offences and the possibility of imposing such a penalty upon the defendant's request, where the guilty person has committed two or more disciplinary offences before the first, if not unlawful, judgment on any of them. The proposed changes, in our opinion, will increase the sense of accountability of judges for the conduct of proceedings, among others. in the context of minimizing its chronicity, which will have a positive impact on the public's assessment of the functioning of the justice system in Poland. Members of the Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle request that the bill be submitted for further work in the Sejm committees. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u245	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party to present our position on the government proposal for a law amending the law on complaint against the right of the party to investigate a case in preparatory proceedings conducted or supervised by the prosecutor and legal proceedings without undue delay and some other laws, print No 851. Today's project results from the need to implement the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights of 7 July 2015 in the Rutkowski case and others against Poland, actions No 72287/10, 13927/11 and 46187/11. The European Court of Human Rights pointed out in its ruling that the national authorities should take appropriate legislative action to ensure that the case law of the national courts determining the length of proceedings complies with the established standards resulting from the provisions of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 and Article 1 13 the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. In addition, it is necessary to implement in the Law on the System of General Courts the recommendations drawn up by the IV round of evaluation of the Group of States against the Council of Europe Corruption, indicating the need for measures to ensure that disciplinary proceedings concerning the improper conduct of judges and prosecutors end before the expiry of the limitation period. The proposed amendment of the Law on Complaints aims to prevent courts from limiting the recognition of a complaint only to the stage at which the proceedings are to be carried out, which will be achieved by modifying the criteria for assessing the determination of the case within a reasonable period of time. The proposed amendment takes into account the Court's guidelines to ensure adequate compensation for the infringement of the party's right to investigate the case without undue delay. This will be achieved through the obligation of the courts to take into account in determining the appropriate monetary sum in relation to the determination of the duration of the proceedings, from the time of initiation until the date of the hearing. The draft proposes that, if the court finds that the length of the proceedings is chronic, the amount of the sum of the money determined should be not less than PLN 1000 for each year of the proceedings, regardless of whether the duration of proceedings is limited to the stage or the various stages of the proceedings, leaving the applicable rules in force, according to which the court grants the sum of money between PLN 2000 and PLN 20,000. Regulation on formal deficiencies in the complaint will also be alleviated. The proposed amendments will contribute to the removal and at least to the limitations found by the Court in the practice of judicial dysfunction in the application of a national protection measure, which is an action for chronicity. As rightly stated in its opinion on the National Judicial Council Act, the text of the proposed article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 raises doubts as to the interpretation that the court, having regard to the action for the length of proceedings, grants a certain amount of money for each year of the proceedings. It would be appropriate to specify for each year the duration established so that monetary compensation is not granted for periods in which the duration has not been established. The proposed amendment to the article is debatable and questionable. 87 § 6 P.u.s.p., introducing the disclosure of statements of property of judges. In accordance with the applicable provisions, the statement of property of the judge shall be examined by the College of the competent tax office. That authority shall, within the framework of its statutory powers, examine the data contained in the statement relating to the property composition of the judge and may, in the event of doubts as to the legality of the origin of the assets disclosed in the declaration, address the notices to the competent services. In our view, this system sufficiently guarantees the transparency of the judges' material situation. If the proposed changes are to improve the fight against corruption, it should be noted that the data to be disclosed to this service already now have access to it. According to the draft disciplinary action against prosecutors and judges, they will not end until the limitation period expires. An extension of the period during which disciplinary action may be initiated against a judge is envisaged from 3 to 5 years after the act has been committed. An extension of the disciplinary limitation from 5 to 8 years is also envisaged in the event of disciplinary proceedings being initiated before the expiry of 5 years. A new disciplinary penalty is foreseen to reduce the basic remuneration of the judge by 5.15% for a period from 6 months to 2 years. Other effects of certain disciplinary penalties have also been identified. The judgment of each disciplinary penalty, in addition to a warning, will result in the refusal of the possibility of being promoted to a higher judicial position for 5 years, as well as the impossibility of taking part in the College of the Court of Justice during that period, of ruling in a disciplinary court and of taking over the office of President and Vice-President, Head of the Centre of the Local Court. All these changes intended to discipline judges and the penalties involved are considered disproportionate and excessive. This is particularly the case for minor offences related to incorrect working methods or lack of time.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u246	A motion to reject the draft law at first reading was put forward in the discussion. We will proceed to the vote on this proposal in the voting block. Because we have acceleration, and the social committee's representatives haven't arrived yet, so that we don't waste any time, we move on to the next point. We proceed with the examination of item 10 of the agenda: Report of the Infrastructure Commission on the Government draft law amending the Road Transport Act (Prints Nos 459 and 874).
