ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u247	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to submit on behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure a report related to the contested draft law amending the Road Transport Act, print No. 459. This law aims to fulfil the obligations imposed by Regulation No 1071/2009 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 October 2009 which laid down common rules concerning the conditions for the occupation of road transport operator and repealing the 1996 Council Directive of the Union as regards the establishment and maintenance of a national electronic register of road transport undertakings authorised by the competent authority to pursue the occupation of road transport operator. I would like to stress, High Izbo, that the bill under consideration has its own history, because unfortunately the deadline for the adaptation of national law has already expired on 31 December 2012. In the meantime, most Member States of the European Union have introduced national registers into their legal order and are exchanging information with countries within the European Union. In particular, I would like to stress that Article. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Regulation No 1071/2009 referred to above by me provides that each Member State shall keep a national electronic register of road transport operators who have obtained authorisation from the competent authority designated by that Member State to pursue the occupation of road transport operator. The processing of the data contained in the register shall be subject to scrutiny by the public authority designated in this respect and the data which contains that register shall be available to all competent authorities of the Member State concerned. In accordance with the Regulation, the Member States referred to here twice before are taking all the necessary measures to ensure that these national registers are implemented. As mentioned earlier, most countries implemented electronic registers a few years ago and the transposition deadline expired in 2012. In the Polish contexts of road transport law, this register will be kept by the main inspector of road transport in the ICT system. The data for this register will be transmitted by the authorities authorised for this purpose, such as the chief transport inspector himself, the starosts, the city presidents on the rights of the county, the voivodships road transport inspectors, police officers, customs officers, the chief of the Border Guard, the chief labour inspector. According to the proposed rules, the data collected in the register of undertakings authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator will be public and publicly available. On the other hand, data collected in the records of serious infringements and records of persons who have been found unable to manage transport operations shall be made available only to authorised authorities and the Internal Security Agency. In the view of the government, the entry into force of the proposed law will have a positive impact on the quality of services provided by road transport operators. Thanks to the data collected in the register, the authorities responsible for the control of traders will be able to respond more effectively and quickly to those committed by certain operators authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator and persons managing road transport breaches of their obligations or conditions for carrying out such transport both within the country and in other Member States of the European Union. This will change - positively, of course - the safety of road transport and freight traffic and create better conditions for fairer competition between carriers. The proposed act will also contribute to strengthening the administrative cooperation of Poland with other countries of the European Union in the supervision of road transport companies which operate e.g. in several EU countries at the same time. The creation of the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will enable Polish drivers who carry out international road services e.g. on the territory of France, the submission of information concerning the registration during the roadside inspection in accordance with the new French provisions which entered into force on 1 July 2016. In this regard, however, there was a very serious problem not only of practical but also of formal law, which was temporarily removed as a result of active policies and appropriate actions within the European government and the Minister of Infrastructure, Construction and Maritime Affairs. The Commission for Infrastructure also made at today's meeting a certain adjustment to the initial submission to the extent that the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will be set up on 31 December 2017 and not, as originally stated, on 1 April 2017. In addition to the chapter on the establishment and keeping of the register, which is added to the Road Transport Act, the project contains very important provisions aimed at ensuring compliance of national provisions with EU Regulations 1071/2009 and 1072/2009, which concern the procedure for the examination of the reputation of entrepreneurs and regulate this procedure. The rules governing this procedure shall determine which infringements will result in the loss of the good reputation of the carrier or transport manager and under which circumstances good reputation may remain intact despite the infringement of the carrier concerned. Information about serious infringements found and the reasons why loss of good repute would be a disproportionate response - and therefore remains intact - will be recorded in that register. Thus, the provisions relating to the examination of good repute are closely linked to the provisions which form the electronic register. The proper design of provisions relating to good repute testing has a significant impact on the proper functioning of the electronic register itself and complies with the requirements laid down in Union law. As regards the scope of matters governed by the law, I would like to draw attention to the fact that the mere process of this project was also complemented by a very intensive consultation between the government party and the associations of international road transport carriers, in order to respond significantly better also to the substantive voices of the market addressed to the government and relating to the issue of the project itself. As part of the work in the Sejm, the first reading of the project took place in May, followed by two meetings on 8 and 9 June. On 22 September, the subcommittee completed its work. Today, the subcommittee's report has been considered. I would like to reiterate that a number of comments made by the social side have been introduced in agreement with the government party. This project has been further analysed in particular in terms of the maximum liberalisation of the draft rules for the examination of the good reputation of road transport operators and transport managers, in order to ensure that the rules comply with EU regulations at the same time. I think that Members working in the subcommittee at the beginning have also contributed to this aspect. It can therefore be said that on each side: and social, and government, and parliamentary, this project has been further examined. The amendments introduced as a result of the subcommittee's work ensure that the draft complies with the provisions of the new Commission Implementing Regulation concerning the procedure for examining good repute which was published in March this year. This is Commission Regulation (EU) 2016/403 of 18 March which supplements Regulation (EC) No 1071/2009 of the European Parliament and of the Council with regard to the classification of serious infringements of Union rules which may lead to the loss of good repute by road transport operator and amending Annex III to Directive 2006/22 of the European Parliament and of the Council. This Regulation provides for a somewhat more favourable procedure for road transport operators than the 2009 Regulation, because it introduces a mechanism for the conversion of these infringements according to their seriousness. Finally, High Sejm, your Marshal, bearing in mind that in those days the Court of Justice of the European Union is taking proceedings against Poland on the conclusion that, by failing to establish the National Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs, the Republic has failed to fulfil its obligations under Article. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 5 of Parliament's Regulation, first of all, there is a need to adopt this draft law urgently and then very active action by the Ministry of Infrastructure and the Chief Road Transport Inspector in order to urgently implement the same electronic register. Once again, I stress that we are debating a document that should be adopted by the Parliament of the previous term of office and implemented. Unfortunately, this did not happen, but it was simply the time that we were given to evaluate and adopt these solutions requested by the government, I think it was used in a very adequate, good way. As rapporteur for the committee, I would like to thank both the social side and the government side, colleagues from the Committee on Infrastructure. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Regulation 1071/2009 provides that each Member State shall keep the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs who have obtained authorisation from the competent authority designated by that State to pursue the occupation of road transport operator. The processing of data contained in the register shall be subject to scrutiny by a public authority designated for that purpose. The relevant data contained in the national electronic register shall be available to all competent authorities of the Member State concerned. In accordance with the Regulation, Member States shall take all necessary measures to ensure that national electronic registers are interconnected and accessible throughout the Community through the national contact points responsible for the exchange of information by 31 December 2012. On the basis of the proposed rules, the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will consist of the following records: firstly, entrepreneurs who are authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator, secondly, serious infringements referred to in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) b of the Regulation, thirdly, persons who have been declared unable to manage the transport operations of the trader until such time as the good reputation is restored. The register shall be kept in the electronic system by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport. Data will be transferred to the register by the authorised authorities: the Chief Inspector of Road Transport, the Elders, city presidents on the rights of the county, provincial road transport inspectors, voivodship police chiefs, the heads of customs chambers, the Chief Labour Inspector, the Road Managers, the Head of the Border Guard. The entry into force of the proposed law will have a positive impact on the quality of the services provided by road transport operators. Thanks to the data collected in the register, the authorities responsible for the control of traders will be able to respond effectively and quickly to those committed by operators authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator and persons managing road transport breaches of their obligations or conditions of carriage by road both within the country and in other Member States of the European Union. This will improve road transport safety and trade in goods and create conditions for fair competition between carriers. Public and public access to the data contained in the register in the register of economic operators authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator will enable the customer of road transport to verify the reliability of transport operators and choose the carrier. It is worth noting that the creation of the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will enable Polish drivers carrying out international road transport in the territory of France to provide information on registration during road inspection in accordance with the new French regulations. All countries that deal with transport issues know that we have had serious problems with this, especially our carriers, so this is another positive thing about this law. The project also provides that the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will be established on 1 September 2017. The above deadline for the creation of the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs results from the need to provide an appropriate time after the publication of the law for the execution of the public contract for the electronic system of the register and the creation of the system by the contractor selected in the public procurement procedure. In view of the fact that proceedings are pending against Poland in the Court of Justice of the European Union as to whether, by failing to establish a National Register of Electronic Road Transport Entrepreneurs and linking it to the national electronic registers of other Member States, the Republic of Poland has failed to fulfil its obligations, we consider that it is necessary to establish such a register and adopt this law urgently. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u259	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Road Transport Act. This register will collect data from road transport operators and will be linked to national registers of road transport operators of other Member States of the European Union. Its task will be to facilitate access to current data of road transport operators, which will directly increase safety in this area by eliminating dishonest entrepreneurs. The construction of a new general register will be based on the existing register kept by the Chief Road Transport Inspector and the records kept in the old towns and cities on the rights of the county. It is foreseen that it will contain records of economic operators who are authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator, records of serious infringements and records of persons who have been found unable to manage the transport operations of the operator until good repute has been restored. The records of the procedated bill detail the data contained in each of these records. These regulations also provide for differentiated access to data contained in the national register, depending on where they are recorded. The data contained in the register of economic operators authorised to pursue the profession, with the exception of date and place of birth and place of residence, shall be made public and the data of the other two records shall be made available only to authorised authorities and to the Head of the Internal Security Agency after the application has been lodged at that time within 30 days of receipt of the application. The creation and operation of the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs entails high costs. However, this is a solution which aims to increase the possibility of supervision of road transport operators. Given the positive aspects of the functioning of such a register and the fact that we are obliged to create such a register by a number of EU directives, the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]

A bill that I have the honour of Today to present to you is the result of a wide consultation and consensus of many environments. The purpose of this Act, which should be known to the public, is not a total ban on trade on Sundays. However, its adoption is intended to civilise trade on this day, following the example of many Western European countries, while respecting the rights, interests of workers, entrepreneurs and consumers. On 2 September we placed over 518 thousand Marshal Marek Kuchciński in the hands of Marshal Marek Kuchciński. signatures, more than half a million signatures of citizens' support for the bill on reducing trade on Sundays. Thousands more signatures are still coming down to us. The total number of signatures that we have managed to collect shows not only how much public support is available for reducing trade on Sundays. It is also a testimony to how important a matter is in society that we will be considering. The bill on reducing trade on Sundays is not an invention of Solidarity and entrepreneurs or any other organisation. It's an answer to the needs of citizens. This civil voice should not be underestimated by the state as having power on behalf of its citizens. We hope that the time when politicians, without blinking an eye, put hundreds of thousands of signatures submitted under civil drafts of laws or referendums, definitely ended with the passing of the previous governing coalition. In the election campaign before last year's parliamentary elections, you all, as you are sitting here, stressed the need to increase the participation of citizens in the exercise of power. Today it's time to say, "I'm checking." High Izbo! The solutions proposed in the citizens' project will reduce trade on Sundays to the minimum necessary. The purpose of this law is to lay down rules for restricting trade in commercial establishments and in traders on Sundays, excluding a few Sundays a year where the need for trade is dictated by a particular need to meet the needs of the population, and to establish rules for the hourly restriction of trade on Sundays, Christmas Eve and the Holy Saturday. In addition, the provisions of the Act provide for derogations from the abovementioned restrictions by establishing economic operators which, according to the scope of services or the form of activity, will be able to operate on Sunday. It is worth repeating to correct the media contradictions that the aim of this law is to limit trade on Sundays, not a total ban. Our goal is not to bring about a situation in which basic foods cannot be bought on Sunday. The project includes a long list of types of commercial facilities that will be able to operate on Sunday. Active on this day will be possible, among others. petrol stations, bakeries, shops located at railway stations and airports or kiosks with press. They will also be able to trade small neighborhood shops, provided that their owner is behind the counter and not the employee employed there. Taking into account the economic interests of entrepreneurs and the needs of consumers, the project also provides for the establishment of seven so-called. commercial Sundays during the year. Stores will be able to be open, among others. on Sundays preceding Christmas and Easter, as well as during the periods during which sales are organized, and before and after holidays. Currently, trade rules in commercial establishments are only regulated in Article. 151 of the Labour Code, § 3 of that provision provides that work on Sundays is permitted in commercial establishments to carry out the work necessary in view of their social usefulness and the daily needs of the population. As a result, trade on Sundays is legally permitted and is a common phenomenon, although it is not possible e.g. consider the operation of construction or clothing shops on Sunday to be necessary to meet the everyday needs of the population. The authors of this project in the course of the analysis of legal and economic considerations considered that changes to the Labour Code, which would restore the ban on work on Sundays, as in the case of holidays, were insufficient. There is a legitimate concern that entrepreneurs would use other forms of work, for example, despite the prohibition of employment on Sundays. an order contract which is not governed by the provisions of the Labour Code. Civil law agreements are already in place in the trade sector, especially in large-scale shops, to reduce labour costs. Only in the first half of 2014 the Labour Inspectors of the State Labour Inspection examined 1,104 contracts, of which 403, i.e. 36,5% , were concluded in violation of the law. For this reason, it is necessary to proceed with the Trade Reduction Act rather than the employment ban on Sundays. The bill also provides for a ban on other sales activities in shops on Sundays, such as e.g. sorting or storage of products, which will effectively guarantee the right to work-free Sundays also for persons employed in peri-sale areas. The distribution, logistics or storage centres providing services for trade are a separate group of entities which are expected to restrict the performance of activities. The project's creators are also concerned with the protection of Sundays free of work in those entities that employ several hundred thousand employees and contractors on a national scale. Of course, the authors of this bill realize that closing on Sundays of various types of logistics and distribution centres, which supply goods to shops located not only in Poland but also in neighbouring countries, is impossible, therefore for people employed in these entities the project provides an exception, i.e. the possibility of hiring on Sundays subject to the consent of the employee and for additional remuneration, and in accordance with the annual limit laid down in the provisions of the Act under procedure in which the work could be provided. These are 24 Sundays for a person, so every other Sunday would be free for these workers. On the one hand, this solution will safeguard the interests of entrepreneurs and on the other hand reduce the need to employ workers on Sundays to the minimum necessary. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, You often refer to European values and standards on this platform. Recently, even a parliamentary circle called European Democrats has been established. So let's look at the issue of trade on Sundays in Western European countries. Trade on that day is prohibited or limited in the vast majority of them. Rules governing this issue are in place, among others. in Austria, Germany, France, Switzerland and even in the UK, Belgium and Denmark. In Germany, the legal protection of Sunday comes directly from the Constitution. In Western countries, trade restrictions on Sundays have not resulted in the mass bankruptcy of shops and dismissals of workers. The citizens of Germany or Austria do not die of hunger or thirst on Sundays. In other words, life goes on there completely normal, even though most stores are closed on Sundays. As a representative of the trade union, I will allow myself to begin with the impact of the regulations contained in the Act on the situation of workers. Currently, in trade, especially in the segment of shopping galleries, supermarkets and hypermarkets, as well as food discounts, it is common to employ workers in an equivalent working time system. This means that Sunday is treated as an ordinary day scheduled in a schedule for which employees do not receive an additional salary. In such a system, instead of Sundays, employees receive a day off on another day of the week. This state of affairs completely disorganizes their personal life and prevents them from living a normal family life. On the other hand, Sunday as a social day is absolutely irreplaceable. Well, so the cashier in the hypermarket gets a day off instead of Sunday. Tuesday when her husband's at work and the kids at school? Because of the unjustified obligation to work on Sundays, not only trade workers, but also their families and, above all, children suffer. In the course of today's discussion on the project, the argument may be that instead of Sundays, it would be better to pay extra for the work on that day. However, such an instrument does not solve the problem in any way and is not accepted by the employees themselves. The own research carried out by the National Secretariat of Banks, Trade and Insurance of NSZZ (Solidarity) in a group of almost 1000 people employed in large-scale shops and discounts has been provided with clear results indicating the need for employees to have a free Sunday. We did it before we started working on the bill to figure out what the employee's expectations were. Only 2% the employees concerned considered that it would be beneficial to pay an additional salary for the work on those days. In addition, the desire for workers to have free Sundays rather than work allowances is dictated by the amount of basic wages in the trade sector. In the case of an average wage not exceeding the minimum wage for work allowances on Sundays, workers would consider that they would be unnoticed and economically insensitive. In addition, there is a great concern that large foreign trade networks, masters in saving on Polish workers, will find a way to compensate themselves for the possible allowance for work on Sundays by lowering other components of the employee's salary. This could result in workers having neither Sundays free nor additional money free. Working on Sundays is also a heavy burden on the health of workers. This is confirmed by the study "The impact of improving psychosocial working conditions on reducing economic costs in companies undergoing modernization and adaptation processes" carried out by the Institute of Labor Medicine in Łódź and the National Commission of NSZZ "Solidarity." These studies show that over 93% The need to work on Sundays has been found to be a burdensome factor and a health risk to the affected trade workers. In addition, the results of the research carried out in the project revealed a high level of stressfulness of trade work. In turn, studies undertaken by the Healthy Work Association confirm that reducing work on weekends, especially on Sundays, is socially desirable for health reasons. The need to work in conflict with potential non-professional roles is a serious stressor. People working on weekends are more likely to experience pain, musculoskeletal problems, insomnia, psychosomatic problems. More often and more often, their emotional exhaustion affects them, and more often they resent them. Difficulty in reconciling work schedules with non-professional life and inability to perform the role of a private parent or spouse even 30 times increase the risk of serious mental illness. What is worth pointing out, the cost of treatment of employees of trade covers the entire Polish society, while the profits associated with this are mainly derived from foreign networks. High Izbo! Contrary to the opinion repeated in the media, reducing trade on Sundays will not result in workers being fired. The Hungarian example points to an increase in trade turnover of more than 5% due to the trade ban introduced in 2015 on Sundays. Although the Hungarian Government finally resigned from free Sundays in trade, the data on growth in turnover is hard evidence that reducing trade on Sundays will not result in a decrease in trade turnover. Where does this increase in turnover come from? Well, before Sunday off, consumers will simply buy in reserve. A study carried out by the National Secretariat of Banks, Trade and Services of the NSZZ.Solidarity in 2015 is also confirmed. We then examined the turnover of the shops on the days preceding Sunday 24 May Green Christmas, i.e. the day legally free from trade, from work. It turned out that the total turnover on Friday and Saturday before Sunday free was about 6% higher than the total turnover on Friday, Saturday and Sunday the following week in which Sunday in trade was a working day. Therefore, since there is no risk of a fall in turnover after the return of free Sundays, there is no indication of any risk of a reduction in employment. Already in the early 1990s foreign trading networks were created in our country conditions of operation incomparably superior to those of domestic entrepreneurs. As a result, foreign networks dominated the retail market in our country, and from year to year they gradually expel Polish entrepreneurs from this market. According to data from Businessweek Poland, the number of stores in Poland decreased by 300 thousand at the end of 2015. In the following six months of this year, 4200 retail outlets disappeared, while in previous years up to 6 and 7 thousand retail outlets were closed down. per year. First of all, small family shops disappear from the market, which cannot compete with foreign markets and discounts. The reduction of trade on Sundays, while making it possible for small shops to operate on that day, provided that their owners are behind the counter, will allow, although to a small extent, a level playing field between small shops and large trading networks. Today, similar regulations apply to holidays, holidays. These days, the owners can also open their shops. As the entrepreneurs themselves point out, this is an opportunity for them to make up for their losses over the weeks, and often the only chance to stay on the market. The aspect of matching the opportunities of domestic entrepreneurs with competitive foreign networks by reducing trade on Sundays should also be highlighted in the context of recent events related to the so-called. commercial tax. The purpose of this tax was to be among others. just supporting small domestic trade. In a situation where the European Commission has blocked trade tax, the restriction of trade on Sundays may at present be the only effective instrument in this area. Polish entrepreneurs, including those operating larger establishments or commercial networks, indicate that they are currently hostages of the market situation. On the one hand, competing with foreign networks, they have to open their shops on Sundays and on the other hand, they are not economically profitable. The turnover achieved on that day does not cover the costs associated with the operation of shops. In an even worse situation there are traders running shops in shopping malls. In their case, the opening of the store on Sundays is linked to severe financial penalties imposed by gallery administrators. The opponents of trade restrictions on Sundays stress that this is an attempt on the freedom to conduct business. If this is what freedom is supposed to look like, that you're either opening a store on Sundays, or we're gonna punish you, and we're gonna let go of the bags, then all we're gonna do is congratulate you. High Izbo! Restoring free Sundays in large-scale trade is also tangible benefits for the state budget. The purchasing activities carried out by consumers will be spread over 6 days rather than 7 days, and the burden of Sunday shopping will be postponed to Friday and Saturday. For the State budget, this means VAT, CIT or PIT revenue at the unchanged level and will increase in the initial period due to the increased turnover of stores on the days following Sunday. There is also no justification for the concern for VAT losses related to the reduction of trade on Sundays. Most purchases are made in the form of chain supplies, where the tax obligation for each supplier arises at the time of delivery, as amended as from 1 January 2014. However, it is possible that the tax obligation arises not at the time of the sale and the delivery to the commercial establishment of the goods with the date set out in the contract. In practice, this means that the omission of Sunday as a delivery day will not mean a tax obligation for suppliers and recipients of goods on those days. A similar situation is possible when the seller forbids the transfer of ownership of the product when it is paid for it. The goods can therefore be stored and the law provides for the use of permits for rapidly spoiling products. It should also be stressed that some of the services in trade are provided on a continuous basis - Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 VAT Act. In conclusion, closing some of the shops on Sundays will not mean a loss to the State budget for VAT. There is also no concern because of the shift of sales turnover to other days, especially the days preceding Sunday. Moreover, the reduction of trade on Sundays is expected to result in a shift in tax burden to catering, entertainment, cultural and recreational sectors due to increased social demand for such services on Sundays. For this reason, the revenue to the budget is expected to amount to about PLN 400 million. In addition, taking into account the above-mentioned 5% increase in turnover of commercial establishments by transferring the purchase by customers to other days of the week, revenues from the sale of food products and non-alcoholic beverages to the State budget are forecast at PLN 1419 million per year. Of course, this situation, as I mentioned earlier, will not last forever, because after a certain period it will return to the level before the law. Speaking of the benefits of implementing the law for the state budget, it is also important to mention the issue of tax avoidance in our country by foreign trade networks and the transfer of earned profits abroad. The secret is that the big trading networks, using e.g. various types of tax optimization instruments, pay very small taxes in our country or even do not pay them at all. This problem has been discussed several times in this House, for example in the context of the bill on the retail tax mentioned above. Today we know that the powerful lobby of large commercial networks has effectively led to the European Commission's blockage of this tax, and at least until new legal regulations in this area are developed, these networks will continue to avoid paying taxes in Poland. In this context it is impossible not to mention once again the positive impact of the Act on reducing trade on Sundays on increasing the chances of competing domestic trade in confrontation with foreign networks. Polish entrepreneurs do not make profits abroad, do not avoid paying taxes, as their foreign competitors do. This is not because they are honest, hardworking people. There's another, much more prosaic reason. Polish entrepreneurs, especially small ones, simply cannot afford to hire a staff of lawyers, tax law experts, who specialise in inventing legal loopholes to avoid paying taxes. Paradoxically, the fact that a large part of Polish merchants, due to market dominance by foreign trading networks today operate on the verge of bankruptcy, is the best guarantee that they pay taxes honestly. [[Applause]] Restoring free Sundays in trade can be the first small step towards reducing the unjustified privileges of foreign trading networks in our country and reducing their foreign profits and tax evasion. As a union, we never avoided talking about it. There was no money to pay, and we knew this was the case for years. We said it loudly, clearly in the media, but no one would listen to us. High Izbo! The proposed bill does not imply a burden on the state budget or the budgets of local government units. The draft law generally does not provide for the need for implementing acts, since the general assumptions are directly based on its content, but it should be appropriate to issue a regulation of the Minister of Finance on the establishment of a template for the level of turnover achieved in relation to the minimum for lead sales laid down in the law. The proposal tightens the sanction for breaching the trade restriction rules, which so far covered only the prohibition of hiring workers on holidays in accordance with the Labour Code. In practice, the control of this ban was ineffective - and in previous terms it was not even the will - due to the limited staff resources of the State Labour Inspection in relation to the number of commercial establishments. Another flaw is the amount of the penalty imposed by PIP inspectors. According to the proposed form of the law, the violation of the trade ban on Sundays will be governed by criminal law. This means more effective respect for the law and more measurable penalties for those who break the ban. The proposed bill on the restriction of trade on Sundays is not contrary to European Union law. As I have already mentioned, the statutory restriction of trade on Sundays is not an exception on a European Union scale. In many European Union countries, trading facilities are closed on Sundays. In the light of the case law of the European Court of Justice, the government of a Member State of the European Union may prohibit and authorise the conduct of commercial activities on Sundays, as this reflects certain political and economic choices to the extent that they are intended to correspond to national or regional socio-cultural characteristics, provided that the restrictive effects of Community trade which may result from restrictions do not go beyond the inherent effects of such rules. Mr. Marshal! It is really high time for Western European countries to start catching up in terms of labour standards. To think that once in Poland what we are fighting for was the norm. It is hard to imagine a better time to regulate civilising trade on Sundays in our country. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u289	Thank you. Małgorzata Zwierzan, Free and Solidarni have the floor. [[Come on, Gretel!]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u290	Mr. Marshal! Minister! (PL) Mr President, I would like to ask you a few questions. Dear guests! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I will present my position on the citizens' proposal for a law on restriction of trade on Sundays. The basic objective of the Act is to establish rules on the prohibition of trade in commercial establishments and in traders on Sundays. The regulations contained in this proposal provide for a trade ban on Sundays, excluding specific Sundays and places where this will be allowed, e.g. fuel stations, pharmacies, florists, and categories of operators who will be able to trade on Sundays and holidays. The Act also aims to regulate working time on Christmas Eve and the Great Saturday. It is also important that the bill contains provisions that clearly define the employment of persons in the area of sales, among others. related to sorting or storage, and the operation of distribution, logistics or storage centres providing services for trade. The projectors considered that employment issues need to be regulated on Sundays by a separate law, since the provisions in the Labour Code alone do not provide adequate safeguards for workers working in trade. Currently, in Poland there are rules that trade on Sundays, with the exception of Sundays on holidays, is legally acceptable and generally universal. The rules proposed by the applicants also tighten the sanctions for breaking the trade ban, which so far covered only the prohibition on hiring workers on holidays in accordance with the provisions of the Labour Code. In the proposed form of the law, the violation of the trade ban on Sundays will be governed by criminal law. This means more effective respect for the law and more measurable penalties for those who break the ban. When considering the relevance of this law, we need to consider two aspects: social and economic. According to the data presented by the designers and daily observation, the majority of employees in the trade are women. They, having very little time for the family a week, cannot pay any attention also on Sundays, because then they work. [[Applause]] The projectors argue that the vast majority of the respondents indicate the need for a free Sunday and do not consider it sufficient to compensate for the day's work or the additional salary for it or any other week's work schedule. I agree with the opinion that the introduction of this law, i.e. the return to the long abandoned tradition of Sunday as a day spent among the closest ones, will lead to the building of relations and strengthening the bond between members of Polish families. As the legislators point out, as a result of the bans in question, the increase in turnover in large-scale stores is projected on the days preceding Sunday, i.e. Friday and Saturday, when consumers will make purchases by promotion. We have such experiences from the days before Christmas, when Poles go to shops massly. It should be stressed that solutions restricting trade on Sundays operate in many European countries: Austria, Germany, Norway, Switzerland, France, Great Britain, Greece, Belgium and Denmark. The draft law was subject to extensive public consultation with employers' organisations and associations. Numerous studies have also been carried out and many conferences have been organised on the subject of the proposed law. The data collected indicate that bringing to the constitutional record the Sunday as a day off from work and promoting the real value of Sunday is one of social expectations. [[Applause]] The economic impact studies also show that the Act will not contribute to the reduction of revenue to the state budget. In view of the above, the Free and Solidarni Circle requests that the citizens' bill be sent to work in committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u469	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In my statement, I would like to raise the issue of the functioning of housing communities. In retrospect, there are shortcomings in the Property Act of 24 June 1994. A lot of conflicts between tenants and problems in the operation of so-called. small housing communities are affected by Article. 19, according to which, if the number of unseparated and unseparated premises still belonging to the previous owner is not more than seven, the rules of the Civil Code and the Code of Civil Procedure for Co-ownership shall apply mutatis mutandis to the management of the joint real estate. In the Civil Code Act of 23 April 1964 in Article. 201 we find a record: for the activities of the ordinary board, the consent of the majority of the co-owners is necessary. In contrast, in the article. 199 we find a provision: the regulation has a common interest and other activities which exceed the scope of the ordinary management board, the consent of all is required. Consequences of the application of Article 199, when the consent of all co-owners is required, are far-reaching. Very often one resident blocks important decisions for the community, even though the majority have a different opinion. This causes conflicts between tenants most often occurring during the planning of renovations. In the assessment of real estate managers who meet this phenomenon on a daily basis, decisions should be taken by the majority of joint owners in communities regardless of the number of residents. Another important problem in the functioning of housing communities is the division of one building into two housing communities or more. There are often conflicts in the settlement of used water or during renovations of the entire building. In the assessment of real estate managers, housing communities should be constructed on the basis of a whole block of buildings with their own water and sewage connections and heating connections. In view of the situation described, I ask the High House to take an interest in it and to introduce legal solutions that will improve the functioning of housing communities. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u487	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 2 October this year We celebrated the 70th anniversary of the death of a Polish scientist, inventor and president of the Republic of Poland Ignacy Mościcki. This great measure was born on 1 December 1867 in Mierzanów. He was from a noble family. His grandfather was a participant in the November Uprising and his father was a participant in the January Uprising. The special ability of Mościcki to mathematical and natural sciences resulted in chemical studies at the Faculty of Chemical Technology of the Ryska University of Technology. At that time, he was associated with an independent socialist movement. In 1892, he and his wife left for London. He continued to study there while working as a worker. In 1896, he met Józef Piłsudski, a year later he went to Switzerland, where he began his scientific career. In Fryburg, he completed a supplementary course. In time, he became a technical manager in one of the companies. The company financed Mościcki's research, which built high voltage capacitors and later conducted numerous experiments. It was Ignacy Mościcki who, becoming a world-famous expert, invented, among others. how to protect the electrical wiring network from electrical discharges. In time, Mościcki missed his homeland and took a job at the Lviv University of Technology, where he took over the Department of Physical Chemistry and Technical Electrochemistry. Since 1922, he has been the Chief Executive Officer of the State Nitrogen Union Factory in Chorzów. After 3 years he took over the Department of Technical Elektrochemy of the Warsaw University of Technology. Well, he was. author of over 60 scientific works and 40 patents. He became a member of the Academy of Technical Sciences and was also a doctor of honoris causa of 17 universities, among others. Sorbonnes and Lviv University of Technology. After the turn of May, Józef Piłsudski was elected President of the Republic of Poland. However, he did not accept this choice while supporting Ignacy Mościcki's candidacy. The third President of the Republic of Poland was sworn in on 4 June 1926. On 8 May, 7 years later he was elected for his next term of office. For a long time, Mościcki, mainly through the current March Constitution, performed honorary and representative functions. The President's broad powers were given only by the April Constitution, which entered into force in 1935. Shortly thereafter, Józef Piłsudski died and there were doubts as to whether Ignacy Mościcki should continue to hold the office of President. Mościcki did not resign, instead he built a political camp around him, in which deputy prime minister Eugeniusz Kwiatkowski played a huge role. After the outbreak of World War II and Soviet aggression on 17 September 1939, together with the government and chief chieftain, President Mościcki was in Romania, where he was interned. On 30 September, he submitted his office and handed it over to Władysław Raczkiewicz. At the end of 1939, he left for Switzerland, where he lived until his death. Ignacy Mościcki died on 2 October 1946 in Versoix near Geneva. In 1993 his remains were brought to Poland and placed in the crypt of the Basilica of St. Jana in Warsaw. The symbolic tomb of Ignacy Mościcki is located in the Alley Deserved in Warsaw's Powązki. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-04-sejm-27-1.u488	Thank you very much. Mr Paweł Szramka, the Kukiz Club15, will now make a statement. You're welcome.

Thanks to the courage and generosity of teachers, even in these terrible years, the school did not stop its activities. The conspiracy education included 462 students, 73 people received a small and 14 people had a high degree. The first postwar school director was Alojza Szubartowski (1945-1966). Other directors were Aleksander Chromiński (966-1990), Tadeusz Kobusiński (1990-1991), Ignacy Czeżyk (1992-1996) - Member of the Sejm, Urszula Giedyk (1996-2004), and from 2004 until now the director is Beata Trzcińska-Staszczyk. Andrzej Duda, President of the Republic of Poland, took the patronage over the celebrations, while the media patronage included Lublin Television, Radio Lublin, Puławy Television and Puławy Radio. The celebration began with the solemn Mass of St. in the church of St. Our Lady of the Rosary for thanksgiving for 100 years of high school existence, as well as for management, teachers, school workers, students, parents and graduates, and all the dead who have created the climate of the Czartorich for decades. The ceremony was concelebrated by His Excellency Fr. Bishop Mieczysław Cisło, and Fr. Cannon, Peter Trela. In his homily, the bishop, thanking him for 100 years of the existence of the I LO, mentioned the role and merits of the Czartoryski family and its prominent representatives and the important tasks of this unique school: deepening the awareness of the great cultural heritage, fidelity of goodness, beauty and truth, traditions and roots, from which they grow the most beautiful values. After Mass in the high school building, a plaque dedicated to the alumni I LO was solemnly unveiled. Fr. A. J. Czartoryski, soldier of the steadfast Marian Bernaciak. - Orlik. A plaque funded by the graduates on the anniversary of the 100th anniversary of the school was also unveiled. Further part of the ceremony took place in the Puławy Cultural Centre of the Chemist House. Among the invited guests were among others. representatives of the Czartoryski family - Princess Barbara Czartoryska, Irena Bylicka, Prince Kazimierz Czartoryski, His Excellency Fr. Bishop Mieczysław Cisło, Małgorzata Sadurska - Head of the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland, Przemysław Czarnek - Lublin Voivode, representatives of Puławy Members - those attended the meeting of the Sejm, representatives of local authorities, representatives of many institutions and workplaces, present and retired teachers, numerous graduates of high school. It was a touching moment to recall all the silhouettes of late directors, teachers and school staff who had created her extraordinary image for 100 years. Their memory was commemorated with a minute of silence, and the young people and their guardians laid a ribbon on their graves and lit candles. During the jubilee celebrations, Princess Barbara Czartoryska received Honorary Citizenship of the City of Puławy. The laudation emphasized her enormous patriotism, commitment and great attachment to Puławy, the tradition of the Czartoryski family and the school itself, whose princess is a great friend. On the other hand, the Chartory Family Association of Herbu Pogoń Litewska for the first time awarded exceptional prizes for students who achieved outstanding results in science. A number of guests gave occasional speeches, including Małgorzata Sadurska - the head of the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland, a high school graduate, read a letter from the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda. The president of Puławy Janusz Grobel gave commemorative medals to teachers who deserved the city. At the end of the ceremony, the House of Traditions was officially opened at the school. The celebrations of the Jubilee ended the performance entitled "J. "Next ex" directed by Beata Burecka and a math competition for graduates and their families. I regret that because of my duties as Members, I could not personally attend these beautiful, unique celebrations. From this point on, I wish my school further success - high-level education of young people and successful graduates in all areas of life. With the help of the school, let them serve their country with dignity. Thank you very much.
